# SD-31 — Open Issues, Blockers, and Operator Rulings Needed

**How this file works.** SD-31 runs unattended. A cycle never stops to ask a question. When a cycle
hits a hard block, needs an operator ruling, or takes a default it wants reviewed, it appends a row
here and keeps working. The operator reads this file on check-in and answers in the `Operator ruling`
column.

**Rules for cycles appending here:**
- Append, never rewrite another entry.
- One row per issue. Give the exact command and exit code, not a narration.
- `Severity`: `BLOCKER` (work stopped on this card), `RULING-NEEDED` (proceeded on a default, operator
  should confirm), `NOTE` (recorded, no action wanted).
- A Structural Exclusion Register proposal (`decisions.md §3`) is logged here as `RULING-NEEDED` with
  a pointer to the proposal — a cycle may propose, only the operator grants.

**THE LIVE ONE, added 2026-08-17 by `SD31-W9-INTEGRATE-001`: race attribution — row 140 below.**
In plain terms: the 7 Core Rulebook races (Dwarf, Elf, Gnome, Half-Elf, Half-Orc, Halfling, Human)
and 9 Bestiary 4 races currently sit on the board under a real, independently-published Paizo
sourcebook (Inner Sea Races, 2015) purely because it is the newest book that CITES their base
traits — even though it never originally PUBLISHED any of them and carries zero of its own base
race declarations (it is 100% `.MOD` citation rows over races other books actually declare). The
question: does a citation-only compendium count as a "printing" under Decision 10's "newest
publish wins" (which would move these races' attribution to Inner Sea Races), or does it stay
excluded the same way `core_essentials` — a PCGen-internal packaging directory, not a real book —
is already excluded under Decision 9 (which would leave them at Core Rulebook / Bestiary 4)? This
is NOT the same question as `core_essentials` (Decision 9 already answered that one: not a book,
excluded); Inner Sea Races is a real, purchasable Paizo sourcebook, so Decision 9's answer does
not mechanically extend to it. Wave 8's `SD31-ATTRIB-003` built the full per-race citation evidence
table this ruling should be made from — `artifacts/SD31-ATTRIB-003-race-evidence.md` §1-§2 — showing
every affected race's citation evidence both including and excluding Inner Sea Races. Full technical
detail at row 140.

**Rows 69/87/95/107 — ANSWERED 2026-08-16, `decisions.md` §7 REFINED (`SD31-D7-PROSE-004`).** The
flat-vs-scaling question this section used to lead with was the WRONG axis; the operator's actual
ruling is UNIVERSAL vs CONDITIONAL: *"+1 size bonus to AC means you need to give a +1 on the AC -
that's computed... if that +4 bonus was ONLY against certain creature types, like with dwarf racial
hatred - that's not a universal bump... if it says acid damage - that's a condition, many things
shrug off acid."* `SD31-D7-PROSE-004` built the real discriminator
(`closure_states_universal_sheet_modifier`, `v06_work_inventory.rs`), retired the four hand-picked
`*_FLAT_MAGNITUDE_PENDING_RULING` name-lists rows 69/95/107 (and wave 7's `CLASS_FEATURE_...`/
`FEAT_...` lists) had built pending this exact ruling, and applied it corpus-wide. Outcome, both
directions, every unit individually re-derived (not estimated): **21 units promoted** (13
`class_feature` + 7 `feat` + 1 `monster_ability` — every one of the previously-parked units, all
genuinely conditional) and **6 units demoted** (`gnome_size`/`grippli_size`/`halfling_size`/
`kobold_size`/`svirfneblin_size`/`goblin_size`, the Small-race size traits — universal AC/attack/
CMB/CMD/Stealth bonuses that must compute, per the ruling's own worked example). Board: 9,780 → 9,795
done (25.3887% → 25.4277%). Full citations, the discriminator's 32-case hand-labelled accuracy proof,
and the corpus-wide false-positive fix (narrowing `UNIVERSAL_MODIFIER_CUES` to `"size bonus"` alone
after 5 false positives surfaced running the retired-list replacement against the FULL corpus) are in
`v06_work_inventory.rs`'s own doc comments and this cycle's `progress.md` receipt. **Row 87's own
`goblin_size` classification was WRONG in this cycle's own dispatch brief** ("grants nothing") — the
real shipped `bestiary:race_trait:goblin_size` row states the identical universal size-bonus shape as
the other 5 and is demoted alongside them; `retro.py correction` emitted, verified by direct `cat` of
`data/corpus/beastiary/race_trait/goblin/goblin_size.json`.

| 1 | 2026-08-15 | SD31-E2-F1-001 | NOTE | `wiring_class::CorpusLines::line()` (`src/rules_core/wiring_class.rs:758`) resolves a unit's corpus row via a **single-level** `dir.join(file)` join. Several books' `.lst` files live in nested subdirectories (`core_essentials/races/<race>/*.lst`, `ultimate_combat/support/*.lst`, `horror_adventures/support/*.lst`, `inner_sea_world_guide/_pfs/*.lst`, `advanced_race_guide/_pfs/*.lst`, `adventurers_guide/support/*.lst`, etc.) — the join silently misses, `CorpusLines::line()` returns `None`, and the unit falls into `ambiguous:no_corpus_line` (D0) even though its row genuinely exists. Re-derived corpus-wide: `python3 -c "import json; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); u=[x for x in d['units'] if x.get('book')!='beginner_box']; ncl=[x for x in u if x.get('wiring_class_reason')=='no_corpus_line']; print(len(ncl))"` → **1,707** units (was documented as 47 in `docs/release/GE-01-legacy-corpus-and-conversion-matrix/artifacts/wiring-class-determination.md`, now stale). Recursive-glob check confirms **100% (1,707/1,707)** of these rows are findable somewhere under the book directory — none are genuinely synthetic/provenance-free. This is **80.9% of the whole 2,109-unit `ambiguous` population**. Not a blocker to SD31-E2-F1 (the ground-truth sample hand-resolved all 40 affected sampled units from the real row instead), but it is load-bearing for Epic 2-F2 (classifier build) and F3 (`ambiguous` dead-end closure): fixing the path join before building/accepting a classifier will move the vast majority of "ambiguous" units to their real class for free, and F3's "genuinely unreachable, propose to Structural Exclusion Register" review must not run against the current, badly-inflated `no_corpus_line` bucket. | Recorded the bug with full re-derivation and worked examples in `artifacts/SD31-E2-F1-ground-truth-methodology.md` §Finding A; hand-labelled the 40 affected sampled units from their real corpus rows (not the engine's `ambiguous` verdict) so the ground-truth sample itself is not corrupted by the same bug. No production code changed (out of this card's scope). | *(needs operator/Epic-2-F2 owner confirmation before any fix lands)* |
| 2 | 2026-08-15 | SD31-E2-F1-001 | NOTE | `wiring_class::signals()`'s scalar/arithmetic scan (`src/rules_core/wiring_class.rs:398-420`) reads the WHOLE field text, not just the magnitude value. Two consequences, both false positives for `derived`: (a) `BONUS:STAT|<ABILITY>|<literal>` fields always contain the ability's own name as the STAT selector (e.g. `STR`,`DEX`,`INT`), which collides with `SCALARS_WORD` regardless of whether the *value* is scalar-dependent — a flat `BONUS:STAT|DEX|2|TYPE=Racial` (`core_rulebook:race_trait:2_dexterity`) or `BONUS:STAT|INT|2|TYPE=Enhancement` (`ultimate_equipment:equipment:staff_of_mithral_might`) is misclassified `derived` instead of `static`. (b) `has_arith`'s unconditional `value.contains('/')` check fires on PCGen's literal `DR:10/Cold Iron`/`CR:1/3` bypass-type notation, not just division (`bestiary:monster:neothelid` compounds both). Confirmed in 3 of 150 sampled units as a clean single-cause misclassification (others carrying the same false-positive signal, e.g. `core_essentials:companion:pig`, still land on the correct class because a genuinely separate formula also fires). Full worked examples in the methodology note §Finding B. | Hand-labelled the 3 affected units `static` with the false-positive mechanism documented per-unit in the ground-truth JSON's `token_evidence` field. | *(informational — feed into Epic 2-F2's classifier acceptance work, not a blocker)* |

| 80 | 2026-08-16 | SD31-E4-F1-001 | NOTE | **`class_feature_owner`'s suffix-only class match produces a genuine cross-archetype false positive, discovered by this cycle's own regen, not fabricated to demonstrate a hazard.** `v06_work_inventory.rs::class_feature_owner` matches a record's group prefix against a modelled class via `group == class \|\| group.starts_with(class+" ") \|\| group.ends_with(" "+class)`. `"Stygian Slayer"` (an ACG Slayer archetype's OWN group name) ends with `" slayer"`, so it resolves `owner = "slayer"` — which is CORRECT here (Stygian Slayer really is a Slayer archetype) and is why `Stygian Slayer ~ Weapon and Armor Proficiency` correctly flips to `grounded` alongside the base `Slayer ~ Weapon and Armor Proficiency` record once my shared explanation id exists (both verified via the guarded regen this cycle, `docs/work-inventory.json` restored after). Confirmed via the same regen diff: `advanced_race_guide:class_feature:skulking_slayer_weapon_and_armor_proficiency` moved `not-started → held` alongside my 2 intended units. This is a **name collision, not a book/subject check** — `class_feature_owner` has no way to see that "Skulking Slayer" is a Rogue thing, not a Slayer thing, because it only ever looks at the group-name STRING. Low material impact today (both land on `held`, not `done`, per row 70; and Skulking Slayer's OWN feature text still would not be quoted, since my code only fires for the character's actually-selected ACG Slayer archetype) but a real, verified classifier hazard the next `class_feature` cycle should know about before wiring a class whose name is a common English-word suffix of another class/archetype's name. | Documented and reproduced precisely; did not attempt a fix (`class_feature_owner` is `v06_work_inventory.rs`, out of this card's file territory, and a real fix needs book-scoping the match, not a quick patch). | *(informational — no ruling needed; worth a `class_feature_owner` book-scoping enhancement in a future `v06_work_inventory.rs` cycle so archetype-name suffix collisions across unrelated classes cannot silently share credit)* |

Corpus-wide the same shape is large: `python3 -c "import json,sys,collections,os; sys.path.insert(0,'scripts/observer'); import pf1e_dashboard_producer as P; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); U=[u for u in d['units'] if u.get('book') not in P.EXCLUDED_BOOKS]; bad=collections.Counter(); n=0\nfor u in U:\n b,k=u.get('book'),u.get('kind')\n if b and k and not os.path.isdir(f'data/corpus/{b}/{k}') and u.get('status') in ('grounded','text-complete','ingested-magnitude','literal-verified','fixture-verified'): bad[(b,k)]+=1; n+=1\nprint(n, bad.most_common(12))"` → **4,007 units are `grounded` or better while their own `book`+`kind` has no `data/corpus/<book>/<kind>/` directory at all** (core_rulebook/equipment_modifier 676, bestiary/monster 326, ultimate_psionics/equipment 326, bestiary/monster_ability 323, ultimate_combat/feat 231, ...). Every one is unverifiable for a **labelling** reason, not a work reason, and the population maps onto the board's 5,596 `held` units. **Corroborating symptom:** the 6 races wave 2's Epic 1 chassis landed (Fetchling, Grippli, Ifrit, Oread, Sylph, Undine) and wave 3's Skinwalker all still read `status=not-ingested`, verdict `not-started`, under `core_essentials:race:*` — real capability work that could not have been credited even if perfect. **Why no instrument caught it:** every check in the chain agrees *with itself* — the inventory says `core_essentials`, the sweep looks for `core_essentials`, finds nothing, reports "not verified", which is true. Nothing ever asks *"the Core Rulebook has seven races, so why does the board say none?"* That needs outside knowledge of what the books contain, and no gate here holds it; even this program's rank-1 detector (ad-hoc commands over source data) was blind, because the source data was self-consistent. **NOTE — partially addressed, not fixed:** `SD31-E6-F3-002` (wave 4) fixed `corpus_literal_sweep`'s attribution enough to move `race` from 0 → 7 done. | 205 | 2026-08-17 | SD31-E6-F8-003 | RULING-NEEDED | **Root-caused why the mythic_adventures/inner_sea_races/horror_adventures feat-gap lanes (`SD31-E6-F2-007`, `SD31-E6-F8-002`) left large `not-ingested` residuals despite landing real gap-table rows: `v06_work_inventory.rs`'s `mod_only_rescue` mechanism (~line 7035) phantom-manufactures 249 standalone `feat` units, corpus-wide, that are provably duplicates of a REAL unit already correctly counted under a DIFFERENT kind.** Mechanism: a `.MOD` row (a PCGen overlay onto an EXISTING declaration, never a new object) inside a feat-classified file is skipped from normal record creation (line ~1769) and stashed in `mod_targets`; afterwards, `mod_only_rescue` (~line 7040) checks whether the row's base name is `declared` under **the file's own nominal kind only** (`Kind::Feat`, since `refine_kind()`, line 1251, only ever reclassifies a `Kind::Race`/`Kind::RaceTrait` file — never a `Kind::Feat` one, regardless of the `.MOD` row's own `TYPE:` facet) — and when it finds no match under that ONE kind, "rescues" it as a brand-new standalone `feat` unit, even though the real object already exists, correctly kinded, elsewhere in the corpus. Confirmed for all 249/249 (zero exceptions): every `mod_only`-origin feat unit has a same-name unit under a DIFFERENT kind somewhere in the inventory (`race_trait` 231, `monster_ability` 16, `class_feature` 2, `companion` 2) — re-derived: `python3 -c "import json,collections; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); byname=collections.defaultdict(set); [byname[(u.get('corpus_key') or u.get('name') or '').lower()].add(u.get('kind')) for u in d['units']]; feats=[u for u in d['units'] if u.get('kind')=='feat' and u.get('origin')=='mod_only']; print(len(feats), sum(1 for u in feats if byname[(u.get('corpus_key') or u.get('name') or '').lower()]-{'feat'}))"` → `249 249`. Three samples hand-verified against the raw `.lst` TYPE: facet (not just name overlap): `mythic_adventures:feat:android_vision` ("Android ~ Vision.MOD", `TYPE:Android MA Racial Trait`) duplicates the real `core_essentials:race_trait:android_vision`; `horror_adventures:feat:grab` ("Grab.MOD", `TYPE:Grab`) duplicates a real `monster_ability`; `inner_sea_races:feat:drow_keen_senses` ("Drow ~ Keen Senses.MOD", `TYPE:KeenSenses`) duplicates a real `race_trait`. Per-book breakdown: `mythic_adventures` 208, `inner_sea_races` 22, `horror_adventures` 17, `adventurers_guide` 1, `ultimate_wilderness` 1 — re-derived: `python3 -c "import json,collections; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); m=[u for u in d['units'] if u.get('kind')=='feat' and u.get('origin')=='mod_only']; print(len(m), collections.Counter(u.get('book') for u in m).most_common())"` → `249 [('mythic_adventures', 208), ('inner_sea_races', 22), ('horror_adventures', 17), ('adventurers_guide', 1), ('ultimate_wilderness', 1)]`. **This is the same defect shape `tests/feat_gap_tables.rs`'s own `the_one_not_ingested_feat_unit_this_lane_deliberately_does_not_close` test already documents for `ultimate_wilderness:"Samurai ~ Mount"` — but that test's comment ("the single not-ingested feat unit this lane leaves standing") is now stale by 248 units; the test's own assertion is still true, only its narrative framing predates the mythic/isr/ha ingests that grew the population.** **Proposed fix, NOT applied**: broaden `mod_only_rescue`'s existence check to search `declared` across ALL kinds for the `.MOD` row's base name (matching real PCGen `.MOD` semantics — an overlay targets whatever declaration already exists, not necessarily one sharing the enclosing file's nominal kind), so a row whose real object is already counted under its correct kind is never phantom-rescued as a second, wrongly-kinded unit. **Estimated impact if applied** (not measured against a live regen — the fix was not made): `feat` kind population 2,610 → ~2,361 (-249); board total denominator 38,521 → ~38,272 (-249, -0.65 pp of the denominator itself). Per this program's Decision 9/10 precedent for denominator-changing fixes (propose, operator rules, THEN apply), this cycle documents and proposes but does **not** touch `mod_only_rescue` or the denominator. `retro.py correction` emitted (subject: `tests/feat_gap_tables.rs`'s own stale "the one" framing). | Root-caused, hand-verified 3 samples against raw corpus TYPE: facets, ran the full-corpus name-overlap corroboration (249/249), documented the exact code location and proposed fix — did not touch `v06_work_inventory.rs`'s `mod_only_rescue` logic or `docs/work-inventory.json`'s denominator. | *(needs an operator ruling: is a `.MOD`-only phantom unit of this shape a genuine "shrink the denominator" the program's Decision-9/10-style propose-then-rule pathway should apply to, given it is a proven cross-kind duplicate rather than real, merely-hard content — same category as Decision 9's `core_essentials` re-attribution, not the same category as declining hard/costly work)* |

The proposed gate is also unimplementable as written: `doneness_verdict(wiring_class, status, kind)` has no access to a unit's signal set, so a `static:literal_magnitudes_only` gate living in Rust stamping code cannot influence a verdict computed in Python from `(class, status, kind)` alone — the cell's own comment says so ("The generator cannot emit this cell … on the next regen the stamp goes away and the unit reads `held` anyway", added by Step 4D blocker B6 for precisely this reason). **Granting this credit therefore requires BOTH a stamp arm AND an amendment to `doneness_verdict`'s `ambiguous` branch (or reclassification of the 102 units out of `ambiguous` entirely).** This makes the dead-end case STRONGER, not weaker: the 102 `{static, derived}` units are unreachable today as well. The refusal below stands on its own merits and is unaffected. **Refused because reading the records one deep refutes it**: `advanced_class_guide:spell:repair_undead`'s magnitude content is `DESC:…heals 1d8 points of damage + 1 point per caster level (maximum +5)` and `advanced_class_guide:spell:silver_darts`'s is `…1d6 points of piercing damage per caster level (maximum 10d6)` — real per-caster-level formulas nothing in this engine evaluates. The stamp that would credit them is `literal-verified` (a byte-equality check on the shipped record), and the only reason it clears `derived`'s rung is the table cell `static\|derived + literal-verified → done`, which is **inert today** because `apply_done_rung_stamps` stamps `literal-verified` on the `Static` arm alone. Activating it for 80 units whose prose states an unevaluated formula credits `done` for having ingested the bytes correctly — Decision 1(a)'s first forbidden item. | Took the refusal, measured the exact population it declined, and left the units at `held`. Wrote the full derivation (including the per-status lower-bound table) into `progress.md`'s `SD31-E2-F3-002-marker` receipt §2-§3. | *(needs an operator ruling: EITHER confirm the refusal — in which case `AT-31-010` cannot be met as written, see row 255 — OR grant the 80-unit credit explicitly, in which case BOTH an `apply_done_rung_stamps` arm AND an amendment to `pf1e_dashboard_producer.doneness_verdict`'s `ambiguous` branch are required, with a permanent test pinning both; a stamp arm alone moves nothing, see the correction above)* |

| 265 | 2026-08-19 | SD31-W15-EQUIPMOD-001 | NOTE | **CONFIRMED and FIXED this cycle: `classify()`'s `Kind::Equipment`/`Kind::EquipmentModifier` arm consulted the equipment consumer-delta probe BELOW the `text-complete` rung, so a unit the probe had actually observed a computed delta for was reported `text-complete` -- strictly weaker evidence -- whenever it also happened to be `text_only` with a real description.** The consultation's own doc comment (`src/bin/v06_work_inventory.rs`, pre-fix) asserted the opposite: *"Consulted even for a text_only-but-undescribed record (below), so a text-and-magnitude-free item the engine somehow still observes a delta for is not demoted underneath its own real evidence."* That was true only for the UNDESCRIBED branch; for the described branch the `text-complete` rung returned first and the observation was never read. The shadowed population is the `.COPY=` alias rows in the `*_equipmods.lst` files: the alias row carries zero magnitude tokens **of its own** (so `text_only` holds -- `magnitude_token_count` is computed on the row alone) while `wiring_class` is determined over the **token closure**, which inherits the base row's real `BONUS:` chain (so the unit reads `computed`, whose only `done` rung is `grounded`). Worked example, proven by running the real engine against the real on-disk corpus rather than by inspection: `core_rulebook:equipment_modifier:adamantine_weapon` (`cr_equipmods.lst:521`, `Material ~ Adamantine ~ Weapon.COPY=Adamantine (Weapon)`) -- `compute_equipment_effects` returns `weapon_enhancement_bonus: Some(TOHIT +1)` for that key and the board still read it `text-complete`. | Moved the probe consultation above every prose rung and pinned it with three tests in `equipment_verdict_rung_tests`: `an_observed_computed_delta_outranks_the_text_complete_rung` (red before the fix, on that exact assertion, `left: "text-complete"` / `right: "grounded"`), plus two negative controls proving the promotion is carried by the observation and by nothing else -- an identical UNobserved unit still reads `text-complete`, and an observation recorded against ANOTHER book does not promote this one (`probe_equipment_effect_wiring`'s `Celestial Shield` book-scoping discipline, unweakened). Measured effect of this row's fix together with row 266's, guarded regen: **12,277 -> 12,302 `done` (31.8709% -> 31.9358%), +25, ZERO demotions, zero denominator change, and zero movement in any kind other than `equipment`/`equipment_modifier`.** | *(no ruling needed -- a rung-ordering defect, fixed against the code's own stated contract.)* |

| 268 | 2026-08-19 | SD31-W15-EQUIPMOD-004 | RULING-NEEDED | **`COST:` -- an item's PRICE -- is a `MAGNITUDE_TOKENS` entry, and on its own it is enough to class a record `computed`. Is a price a magnitude for wiring-class purposes?** `wiring_class::signals()` states its own intent plainly: *"A `computed:` signal only matters if there is a magnitude for it to govern. A guard or a choice on a row with no magnitude token gates TEXT, and text is `display` work under the standing ruling."* But `COST:` is in `MAGNITUDE_TOKENS`, so a row whose only number is its price and which carries an ordinary purchase prerequisite (`PRETYPE:`) satisfies that gate and lands `computed:pre_guard`. Worked example, verbatim from the corpus: `advanced_class_guide:equipment_modifier:exclusionary_amf` (`acg_equipmods.lst:122`) resolves to `COST:3750` + `PRETYPE:1,EQMOD=Special Ability ~ Amulet of Mighty Fists Base` + `SPROP:can choose not to include target in channel ability for next minute; maximum of Wisdom bonus plus enhancement bonus exclusions` -- and no `BONUS:` chain at all. The `PRETYPE` gates whether the modifier may be APPLIED, not any computation; the mechanic is prose and its only number is stated conditionally in that prose. **194 `equipment_modifier` units are in exactly this shape** (see row 267), and `computed`'s only `done` rung is an observed consumer delta they can never produce. This is `decisions.md §7`'s condition 2 ("nothing to compute") asked about a price, and §7-REFINED's universal-vs-conditional axis does not answer it -- a price is neither a universal modifier to a value the sheet computes nor a conditional bonus. NOT acted on: three candidate answers move the same units three different ways (`display` -> `text-complete` -> **done**; `static:literal_magnitudes_only` -> **done only after `corpus_literal_sweep` byte-verifies the literal**, a real and already-working rung for this kind, 35 units hold `literal-verified` today; or unchanged -> permanently unreachable), and choosing one on my own reading of "nothing to compute" is precisely the self-serving reclassification Decision 1(a) forbids. | Measured it and left every unit exactly where it was. Note the blast radius is NOT confined to this kind: `wiring_class::signals()` reads `COST:` for every kind, so any ruling here must be sized corpus-wide before it is applied. | **RULING NEEDED:** for wiring-class purposes, does a record's `COST:` (its price) count as a magnitude that a `PRE`/`CHOOSE` guard can govern? If it does not, does a price-only record read `display` (prose done-bar, `done` on its description alone) or `static` (a real shipped literal, `done` only once the literal sweep byte-verifies it)? |
| 269 | 2026-08-19 | SD31-W15-EQUIPMOD-005 | NOTE | **Two instruments disagree about the same record's `wiring_class`, and nothing reconciles them.** `data/corpus/<book>/equipment/**/*.json` carries a baked `wiring_class` field written by the cache generator; `docs/work-inventory.json` carries one computed live by `v06_work_inventory` over the token closure. Across the 167 `computed`+`text-complete` `equipment_modifier` units this lane examined, **154 of 167 disagree** -- corpus JSON says `display`, the live inventory says `computed` (e.g. `advanced_class_guide:equipment_modifier:exclusionary_amf`: corpus `display:no_magnitude_token`, inventory `computed:pre_guard`). The live value is the board's source and is the closure-aware one, so the BOARD is not wrong; the baked one is stale and no gate compares the two. Related but distinct: `magnitude_token_count` is computed on the unit's OWN row while `wiring_class` is computed over the token CLOSURE, so for every `.COPY=` alias row the two halves of one verdict read different evidence -- which is also why 191 `computed` `equipment_modifier` units sit in `unmeasurable` (190 after this cycle's fixes) (`text_only` true on the alias row, no `DESC:` anywhere in the closure) rather than in a measurable cell. | Reported, not fixed. | 270 | 2026-08-19 | SD31-W15-EQUIPMOD-006 | RULING-NEEDED | **645 of the 1,580 `equipment_modifier` units (40.8 %) are PCGen `.COPY=` aliases whose base identity is ALSO a unit of the same book — one object standing in the denominator twice, and `decisions.md §10`/`§13`/`§14` do not reach it, because every one of them is about the same object printed in TWO BOOKS.** A `<Base identity>.COPY=<Alias>` row mints a second inventory unit whose `corpus_key` is the right-hand side; the alias inherits the base row's whole token closure and carries `VISIBLE:NO`. Worked examples, verbatim from the pinned oracle: `cr_equipmods.lst:521` `Material ~ Adamantine ~ Weapon.COPY=Adamantine (Weapon)` and `cr_equipmods.lst:537` `Material ~ Adamantine ~ Weapon.COPY=ADAMANT_WEAP` — so the CRB's single adamantine-weapon material is **three** units (`material_adamantine_weapon`, `adamantine_weapon`, `adamant_weap`), all resolving through `equipment_id_resolve` to the one record `Material ~ Adamantine ~ Weapon`, which is proven by running the engine: `compute_equipment_effects` returns `resolved_key: "Material ~ Adamantine ~ Weapon"` for all three keys. Derivation: for every `origin: copy` unit, read its own `.lst` row, take the text before `.COPY=`, and test whether that identity is itself a unit of the same book — 646 alias units, 645 with a live base (the one exception is an alias whose base is not separately enumerated). | **Measured only. Nothing was excluded, proposed for exclusion, or moved** — the register is PROPOSED-NOT-APPLIED and frozen, deferral is not available, and a same-book alias is a different question from the cross-book supersession the freeze is about. Recorded here so the figure exists before anyone needs it. | **RULING NEEDED:** under `§14`'s provenance vocabulary, what status does a same-book `.COPY=` alias pair carry? The three candidates, with what each implies: **(a) `duplicate`** — the alias is an identical reprint of the base within one book, base is `origin`, alias leaves the denominator (this kind alone would shrink by ~645, and the same shape exists in `equipment`, so size it corpus-wide first). **(b) `variant`** — a distinct derived object in its own right, both stay, denominator unchanged and the status merely records the lineage. **(c) neither — a packaging artifact**, i.e. `.COPY=` is PCGen's short-key mechanism rather than a publishing act, so the alias is not a printing of anything and should never have been enumerated as a unit at all (this would be an INVENTORY-ENUMERATION change, not a provenance one, and would need `§3`'s signature). Note (a) and (c) both shrink the denominator, which is why this is an operator call and not an engineering one. |

Sized precisely: **1,562 not-done `class_feature` units** sit under a group whose adjective+word shape can never satisfy the exact-match check (command, groups without a real `" ~ "` separator excluded, one word matched per unit -- first suffix match wins, no double count: `python3 -c "import json,collections; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); U=[u for u in d['units'] if u.get('kind')=='class_feature' and u.get('status') in ('not-ingested','not-started','unknown')]; dead=['Grand Discovery','Advanced Talents','Hex','Revelation','Blessing','Bloodline','Domain','Order','Mystery','Curse','Spirit','Animal Focus','Arcane School']; groups=[(u.get('corpus_key') or '').split(' ~ ')[0] for u in U if ' ~ ' in (u.get('corpus_key') or '')]; c=collections.Counter(next((w for w in dead if g!=w and g.endswith(' '+w)), None) for g in groups); del c[None]; print(sum(c.values()), c.most_common())"` -> 1562 total; by word: Bloodline 452, Domain 422, Mystery 234, Hex 220, Spirit 88, Blessing 82, Animal Focus 35, Arcane School 17, Order 9, Revelation 3). Independently reproduced with a second, unrelated implementation (`awk` over a plain corpus_key-group text dump, not the Python `dict`/JSON path) -- both agree exactly at 1562, per this program's own two-independent-implementations discipline for a board-sizing figure. This means row 152's own "472 units (5.6%) already touch a wired pool" figure is an OVER-count of what the registry can structurally reach today — several of those 28 "covered" pools were never reachable at all, registry aside. | Characterized and quantified; did not fix (`v06_work_inventory.rs` is lane 2's file, out of this card's territory — the same boundary row 167 respected). | *(needs a lane-2 dispatch: change the 13 dead entries' `group` matching from exact-equality to a suffix/pattern match — e.g. `group.ends_with(&format!(" {g}")) \|\| group == *g` — and re-derive the reachable population; 1,562 units is the sizing floor for that fix alone, before any new primitive wiring)* |

Fixed via a new `diagnostic_id_names_feature()` that strips only the fixed `"class_feature"` prefix token before searching — narrower than an exact-segment match, which would have regressed established, already-shipped matches this file has never gamed (e.g. `"Bloodrager ~ Bloodrage"` legitimately matches its class's catch-all only because `"bloodrage"` is a substring of the OWNER token `"bloodrager"` itself; `"Slayer Talent ~ Feat"`/`"Ninja Trick ~ Feat"` legitimately match because `"features"` genuinely contains `"feat"` — neither substring lives inside the stripped prefix, both keep working). Checked the fix against all 35 pre-existing `class_feature` `deferred-with-reason` units and found it is **not** a pure no-op there: 34 of 35 are unaffected, but `"Phrenic Slayer ~ AS"` (`feature_slug == "as"`) LOSES its match — its old match was itself the identical spurious-substring shape (`"as"` is a substring of the fixed prefix's own `"class"` token, `cl-AS-s`), not a deliberate one, and `"Phrenic Slayer"` is itself an archetype-qualified group (not the bare `"Slayer"` class), so attributing Slayer's base-class catch-all diagnostic to it was independently already in tension with the `decisions.md §10` AMENDMENT the diagnostic-quote branch has no `group == owner` guard against at all (a broader, separate, NOT-fixed-here gap, flagged for a future cycle). Reported here rather than silently absorbed into "zero regressions" — this fix does not gain-only. **The same universal `"class_feature"`-contains-`"feat"`-or-`"as"` false-positive shape can recur for any future class whose diagnostics happen to sort a spurious match before (or instead of) its own catch-all — the fix is general (strips the prefix for every owner, not only PU), so it will not need re-discovering per class, but the diagnostic-quote branch's total absence of a `group == owner` guard remains a standing, unfixed gap this cycle did not have scope to close.** Measured, guarded regen (before/after every unit individually diffed by id, `docs/work-inventory.json` restored per the wave rule): **85** total `class_feature` moves, zero moves in any other `kind` — `not-started`→`done` 45, `unmeasurable`→`not-started` 21, `not-started`→`held` 12, `not-started`→`deferred` 4, `unmeasurable`→`deferred` 1 (`"Unchained Rogue Talent ~ Feat"`, now correctly citing its own class's catch-all), `deferred`→`done` 1, **`deferred`→`not-started` 1** (`"Phrenic Slayer ~ AS"`, the regression above). | 174 | 2026-08-17 | SD31-E6-F10-002 | NOTE | **Refines `OPEN-ISSUES.md` row 128's `description`-vs-`DESC:` coverage-gap finding with a root cause, and explains why the extension named there (row 91/103's remaining scope) is NOT safely buildable as a same-cycle add-on — confirms row 103's own caution rather than contradicting it.** Dry-run analysis (no code committed, no gate touched) over every shipped `lst_token` record carrying both a literal `DESC:` token and a `data.description` field: **16,289 corpus-wide, 2,594 (16%) byte-mismatch** (independently re-derives row 128's 2,186/16,817 figure, same shape, different snapshot). Traced the FIRST several mismatches by hand (`data/corpus/core_rulebook/equipment/arms_armor/net_base.json` et al.) and found the root cause: PCGen's `.lst` `DESC:` fields encode a literal newline as `&nl;`, and the shipped `description` field correctly DECODES it to a real `\n` for player display — a deliberate, correct transformation, not a defect. Re-ran the same comparison normalizing `&nl;` -> `\n` first: mismatch count barely moves (2,594 -> 2,404 corpus-wide), and the residual is NOT uniform either — a second sample (`occult_adventures/class_feature/medium/*`) shows the OPPOSITE convention: `description` there still carries the raw, UNDECODED `&nl;` literal. **Confirmed empirically: different kinds/ingest paths handle PCGen's escape-entity encoding inconsistently, exactly the cross-kind risk row 103 named** ("a wrong reconstruction rule would itself manufacture false findings across other kinds' differently-shaped description conventions"). (3) A 3rd defect, found by the FULL gate rather than proposed: 2 bare PFS organized-play legality OVERLAY rows (`_pfs/pfs_b2_equip_arms_armor.lst`'s `Maul of the Titans`, `_pfs/pfs_b3_equip_arms_armor.lst`'s `Ranged Cannon` — every row in a `_pfs/*.lst` file is shaped `<bare name>\tTYPE:PFSNotLegal\t!PRECHARACTERTYPE:1,PC`, never a new declaration) were shipping as spurious second catalog entries citing a DIFFERENT, real row's line, because their bare name didn't match that real row's own archetype-qualified `KEY:` and so slipped the `seen` dedup — caught by `tests/v06_corpus_trap_report.rs`'s `ingested_record_keys_match_their_cited_line`, fixed by teaching `is_non_record_line` to recognize a content-free `TYPE:PFSNotLegal` row (146 corpus-wide occurrences checked first: only 11 also carry real content, so the predicate excludes on ABSENCE of `COST:`/`WT:`/`DESC:`/`SPROP:`/`EQMOD:`, not presence of the flag alone). **Net result: 424 real JSON records written, 382 of the board's own equipment/equipment_modifier `done` units this cycle (11,229 -> 11,611, 29.1503% -> 30.142%), zero denominator change, corpus_literal_sweep CLEAN before and after, guarded regen zero stamp loss.** | Fixed all three defects at the root, TDD (23 new unit tests across `gen_equipment_gap_tables.rs`), full downstream count sweep (`equipment_resolver.rs`, `equipment_catalog.rs`, `character_hub.rs`, `tests/equipment_gap_tables.rs`, `reach_gate.rs`'s append-only registration lists, and 8 books' `LICENSE.json` `records_processed` restated with a screening-method-note citation each). | *(no ruling needed for the 3 fixes shipped — engineering follow-up for the 5 PI-blocked books: build a per-row redact/exclude pass for `gen_equipment_gap_tables.rs` mirroring `gen_cache_equipment_gap`'s existing per-record name-exclude, so those ~394 units become reachable without weakening the blacklist hard-stop for a genuinely PI-dense book like Inner Sea Gods.)* |

| 306 | 2026-08-19 | SD31-W17-SPELL-COMPANION-CF-001 | NOTE | **`spell`'s 248 held units investigated in full: the 45-unit `derived`+`grounded` seam is a KNOWN, already-logged, already-RULING-NEEDED population (row 296/`decisions.md`) and was not re-litigated; the remaining 203 (118 `derived`+`ingested-magnitude`, 60 `ambiguous`+{`grounded`/`ingested-magnitude`/`text-complete`}, 25 `derived`+`text-complete`) were traced to their root cause instead, per this wave's dispatch instruction that finding this out is worth more than a handful of seam units.** Two structurally DIFFERENT causes, corpus-wide census: **(1) 167 of 203 (82%) are non-`core_rulebook` spells (APG 53, ISG 58, UM 22, UI 11, ACG 10, OA 8, UC 5) and are structurally UNREACHABLE by the ONLY consumer-delta instrument this engine has for the `spell` kind** — `spellbook::compute_spellbook_coverage` hard-codes `spell_id_resolve(&selection.spell_id, RuleSetId::Crb, corpus)` (`src/rules_core/spellbook.rs`), and every one of its nine per-school resolver functions (`abjuration::resolve_abjuration_spell_effect` etc.) dispatches against `crb::spell_list::SPELL_LIST` — a SINGLE, CRB-only table. So a non-CRB spell's record resolves (`spell_id_resolve` matches by bare NAME against whichever single-book corpus was loaded, book-agnostic), but the per-school table lookup that would produce a level/DC always misses, landing `NoTableEffect`. Independently confirmed by running the probe directly: `cargo run --locked --bin v06_work_inventory -- --spell-probe` → `core_rulebook: no_casting_class_has_it 41, wired 623`; every OTHER of the 6 examined books (ACG/APG/ARG/UC/UI/UM; OA and ISG are not even in the probe's own book loop, a second, narrower structural gap — `probe_spell_effect_wiring`'s `OBSERVABLE_BOOK_DIRS`/`engine_book_for_corpus_dir` pairing was never widened past the original 5-6-book roster) shows `no_casting_class_has_it`/`no_table_effect` for its ENTIRE population, 0 `wired`. A second, independent structural layer compounds this: `SPELL_PROBE_CASTING_CLASSES` (the probe) and `casting_ability_for_class` (`spellbook.rs`, the real engine) both hard-code exactly the same seven CRB classes (wizard/cleric/druid/bard/sorcerer/paladin/ranger) — no Witch/Oracle/Alchemist/Summoner/Magus/Investigator/Bloodrager/Skald/Occultist/Medium/Mesmerist/Psychic/Spiritualist/Kineticist casting class exists in this engine at all, so even a CRB spell exclusive to one of those classes cannot ground (this is where CRB's OWN 41 `no_casting_class_has_it` units come from). Widening either layer is a real, multi-book/multi-class capability build (new per-book school tables plus new casting-class ability mappings) — `spellbook.rs`/`rules_tables/{apg,acg,advanced_race_guide,ultimate_magic,ultimate_intrigue,occult_adventures,ultimate_combat,inner_sea_gods}` are all outside this lane's granted file scope ("spell tables/fixtures", not the engine) and outside a single cycle's proportionate size. **(2) The remaining 36, all `core_rulebook`, split (`wiring_class`, `status`) 32×(`ambiguous`,`grounded`) + 4×(`derived`,`ingested-magnitude`; the 3 `Burning Hands (<Energy>)` metamagic-substitution names plus `Summon Monster III (lantern archon only)`, none of which is a real distinct `SPELL_LIST` entry, so the probe never reaches `grounded` for them at all) — the 32 are a DIFFERENT, already-partially-known gap: `wiring_class::signals()`'s `ambiguous:prose_scaling_phrase` rule.** A spell like Wish (`"A single wish can aid one creature per caster level"`) states its ONLY magnitude in free `DESC:` prose containing a literal phrase (`"per caster level"` etc.) with no structured `RANGE:`/`DURATION:` token at all, so the classifier correctly refuses `static`/`display`/`derived` and lands `ambiguous` — which, per `_doneness_verdict_uncapped`'s own documented lower-bound rule, caps even a `grounded` consumer-delta observation (all 32 ARE `grounded` — the consumer probe DID observe a real level/DC for each, since they ARE `core_rulebook` and ARE on one of the seven casting-class lists) at `held`, never `done`. This is the SAME materially-larger "DESC-formula parser" gap row 296/wave-16's own `class_feature`/`spell` receipts already named for the 31-unit `derived`+`grounded` DESC-prose-formula population — not a new finding, but now confirmed to ALSO explain 36 more units under a different `(wiring_class, status)` cell. | Measured and traced to root cause, corpus-wide, with exact file/function citations and a live probe run rather than assumed from the wave-16 census label; did not attempt to widen `spellbook.rs`'s CRB-only dispatch or add new casting classes (multi-book/multi-class engine capability, outside this lane's file grant and outside proportionate single-cycle size); did not touch the 45-unit ruled-pending seam. | *(RULING-NEEDED, scoping input rather than a value ruling: is widening the spell-effect consumer-delta probe beyond `core_rulebook` and beyond the seven CRB casting classes worth a dedicated multi-cycle capability epic? It is the single largest structural gap this wave found in ANY kind — 167 units immediately, growing corpus-wide as more books' spell/class content ingests — but sizing it correctly needs a `spellbook.rs`-owning cycle, not this lane's `spell tables/fixtures` grant. The 36-unit `ambiguous:prose_scaling_phrase` half is the SAME DESC-formula-parser gap already logged at row 296/`decisions.md` and needs no separate ruling.)* |
